# Features — gisik-nokma

Legend: ✅ user-observable · ⚙️ internal subsystem · ? inferred/unverified

## 1. Reference base

- ⚙️ **Vendored tracker snapshot** — the convenience-corner project tracker, verbatim at upstream commit `24e2240`: the four-view static page (Focus, Board, Backlog, Calendar), its authored attention file, its generated data snapshot, and the generator with its 73 tests. Frozen; provenance and the freeze rule in `reference/convenience-corner-tracker/PROVENANCE.md`. `reference/convenience-corner-tracker/`
- ⚙️ **Base design documents** — the three upstream documents that designed the base: the tracker (2026-08-18), the calendar view (2026-08-19), and the calendar's implementation plan. `reference/design-docs/`

## 2. Product design

- ⚙️ **Product design spec** — the brainstorm's approved output: audience (non-technical operators), the honesty contract, event-sourced architecture with a shared rule engine, workflow engine and builder, offline sync with contested claims, permissions and visibility, views and search, testing discipline, and the two dogfooding targets. Approved and merged (PR #1). `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-gisik-nokma-product-design.md`
- ⚙️ **Implementation plan series** — eleven task-by-task plans covering the whole build, ledger through deployment and dogfooding, written in parallel from one pinned contract and then reconciled. Reviewed and merged via PR #2; execution in progress, plan by plan. `docs/superpowers/plans/`
- ⚙️ **Series coordination document** — the single authority for cross-plan interfaces, naming, and ownership, and the record of every decision taken at review: where a plan disagrees with it, it wins. `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-20-00-series-coordination.md`

*(Product feature sections begin once implementation starts. Sections 3–13, and the matching `test-cases/` buckets, are already reserved one per plan by the coordination document. The brainstorm that produced the spec started from `docs/brainstorm-kickoff.md`.)*

## 3. Foundation & event ledger

- ⚙️ **Monorepo workspace** — pnpm workspaces + Turborepo, TypeScript strict, Node 22, ESM, Vitest; packages `@gisik-nokma/domain`, `@gisik-nokma/rule-engine`, `@gisik-nokma/server`, `@gisik-nokma/ui`, `@gisik-nokma/app-core`, `@gisik-nokma/sync-client`, `@gisik-nokma/web`. The gate is one `build`/`test`/`typecheck` triple per workspace package — a count re-derived by running it, never written down here, because a number in prose has a shelf life that ends at the next package. `build.outputs` names `.next/**` beside `dist/**`, without which turbo would report a cache hit for a Next build whose output it never stored; `globalEnv` names `SERVER_ORIGIN` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SERVER_URL`, because the latter is BAKED INTO the browser bundle and a cached build compiled against a different one would otherwise be served as if it were current. Root configs `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `turbo.json`, `tsconfig.base.json`; dev Postgres via `docker-compose.yml`.
- ⚙️ **CI pipeline (skeleton)** — GitHub Actions workflow on every pull request and push to `main`: a single job installs via pnpm, brings up a Postgres 16 service container matching `docker-compose.yml`'s user/password/port, and runs the turbo gate (`build`, `test`, `typecheck`); IST convergence rides the same per-test-database `ALTER DATABASE` the test harness already runs, not a container-level override. The independent-CI precondition for the coordination doc's auto-merge protocol. Scoped to what Plans 1–2 landed — lint, `check:env` (Plan 11 T6), the client half of the fixtures suite and the dual-client job (Plan 10), the e2e job (Plans 4/6), and the interim-plumbing gate (Plan 10) land with their owning plans; Plan 11's own catalogue task owns the full pipeline under §13. `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
- ⚙️ **Event envelope & vocabulary** — the exact `EventEnvelope` contract with zod validation, ULID helpers, and the versioned event-type registry; pure `parseEvent` refuses unknown types, unknown versions, invalid payloads, malformed envelopes, and — for a registration that declares an `entityKind` — an envelope whose `entity.kind` differs (`entity-kind-mismatch`; layered with the rule engine's own kind checks by design, since fixtures and offline replicas bypass `parseEvent`). Provisional seed vocabulary: `workspace.created` v1, `workspace.renamed` v1; the registry now also holds `workflow.defined` v1 (Plan 2). `packages/domain/`
  - ⚙️ **An assistant's write is a distinct provenance, not a flavour of the web's** — `ORIGINS` carries `'mcp'` alongside `'web' | 'mobile' | 'desktop' | 'server'` (MCP plan Task 0, owner ruling 1). In a product whose stated purpose is a board that cannot lie about its own history, a write claiming `'web'` when a machine made it is the history lying about who acted. `Origin`, `z.enum(ORIGINS)` and the permission gate's `CLIENT_ORIGINS` all DERIVE from the array, so the member was the whole edit — the gate gained an `mcp` case it did not have to be told about, and no migration was needed (`origin text NOT NULL`, no `CHECK`, no Postgres enum, verified across every migration). The rule engine's fixture harness now imports `ORIGINS` instead of re-spelling it. Case 3.163. `packages/domain/src/envelope.ts`, `packages/rule-engine/src/fixtures/harness.ts`
- ⚙️ **Contest event vocabulary** — `claim.contested` v1 and `claim.contested_resolved` v1, the two types that make a sync-time refusal into ledger history instead of a silent drop (spec honesty invariant 5, coordination amendment 16). The contest carries the refused claim whole (`originalEvent`, a full `SubmittedEvent`) and is refused if the claim arrives without its `payload` — `z.unknown()` makes that key optional in zod, so the envelope door's own payload-presence check (Plan 4's amendment) is what stops the ledger from recording that something was claimed without recording what. `null` and `{}` still pass: an empty claim is a claim, an absent one is not. Resolution is `redone` | `converted-to-comment` | `discarded`, and the first two must link the event they produced — a redo without its replacement id, or a conversion without its comment id, is refused. Both types bind `entityKind: 'ticket'` in the registry, so a contest filed against a workflow or a workspace is refused at the door with `entity-kind-mismatch` rather than appended to an append-only table where it could never be corrected. Alongside them: `renderContestedClaimText(payload)`, the one attributed, IST-dated, rule-naming sentence a banner shows and a "convert to comment" writes into the ledger forever, and `plainTextToDoc(text)`, the inverse of `docToPlainText` — the minimal ProseMirror doc that conversion needs because bodies are PM JSON. `packages/domain/src/events/contest-events.ts`, registrations in `packages/domain/src/vocabulary.ts`
- ⚙️ **Client envelope builder** — `newSubmittedEvent(...)`, the one place a client mints an event: it fills `id` (ULID), `version` and `clientTime` and copies the rest of the caller's claim. `serverTime` and `seq` are absent by construction, because they are the server's to assign at ingest and a client that invented them would be asserting facts it does not own. `packages/domain/src/submit.ts`
- ⚙️ **IST day boundaries** — `istDayOf(instant)` and `istDayBounds(day)` against the fixed +05:30 offset (India has no DST), plus `IST_TIME_ZONE`: the single origin for where a day starts. "Due today", "overdue", calendar-cell assignment and day counts across the whole product resolve here, never from the server's locale or the viewer's device. `packages/domain/src/ist-day.ts`
- ⚙️ **IST instant rendering & clock skew** — `formatIstInstant(instant)` is the ONE way the product prints an absolute instant to a person (`'21 Aug 2026, 00:00 IST'`). Its day comes from `istDayOf`, so a printed timestamp can never disagree with the calendar cell the same instant lands in; it does constant-offset arithmetic with no `Intl` and no date library, because Hermes on mobile does not reliably ship a timezone database and a silent fallback to the device's zone would be the offline copy disagreeing about when things happened. Anything that is not a well-formed, real ISO instant travels back verbatim rather than as an invented time: unparseable, year-extended, calendar-impossible (`2026-02-30`), a day claim like `2026-08-20` that `Date.parse` would call midnight UTC, or a string with no stated offset that would otherwise be read in the device timezone. `IST_CLOCK_NOTE` is the line a surface puts beside a time so a collaborator in another timezone knows which clock they are reading. Beside them, `clockSkewMs` / `GROSS_CLOCK_SKEW_MS` (10 min) / `hasGrossClockSkew` compute skew from the two clocks the envelope already keeps: DERIVED, never stored and never projected, so the flag is identical wherever it is computed and an unparseable client claim counts as gross rather than as zero. `packages/domain/src/clock-skew.ts`
- ⚙️ **Plain-text document rendering** — `docToPlainText(doc: unknown): string`, a dependency-free ProseMirror/Tiptap JSON walker (coordination amendment 12): total on junk input, mention nodes `{type:'mention', attrs:{userId, label?}}` render as `@label` falling back to `@userId`, image/attachment nodes render as nothing. Pulled forward as pre-work (Plan 5 Task 1) because `proj_search` (Plan 5), Plan 7's editor, and Plan 9's notifications all consume it; landed here in §3, and Plan 5's own catalogue task (Task 16) has since run — 2026-09-02 — and deliberately LEFT it here rather than relocating it to §7, because the bullet belongs beside the ledger material it renders. Its TEST CASES are 7.1, 7.2 and 7.159 in `test-cases/07-api-realtime-search.md`, where §7's reservation held: `doc-text.test.ts` had been cited by no `test-cases/` file at all until that task ran. `packages/domain/src/doc-text.ts`
- ⚙️ **Design tokens — the one colour origin** — `primitives` (hex palette) resolved into 26 semantic `TOKEN_ROLES` for light AND dark, emitted as `--gn-*` CSS custom properties (`themeStylesheet()`: light default, dark by explicit `data-theme="dark"` or by OS preference when unchosen). The origin declares its own `CONTRAST_PAIRS` and the suite verifies all 7 pairs in both themes — a palette edit that breaks readability fails the build. Coordination amendment 11; no colour literal is legal anywhere else in any client package. **The scanner enforcing it judges POSITION, not shape** — comments are stripped over the whole file, and a hex counts only where a colour is applied: a CSS declaration value, or a string in script. It has to: `#270` — a PR number in prose — is three hex digits and therefore a legal CSS shorthand colour, so no tightening of the pattern could separate the two, and on 2026-09-07 it turned `main` red. The value-position rule also stops a CSS **ID selector** (`#abc { }`) being read as a colour, which was latent from the start. **What it deliberately no longer catches: a colour written inside a comment** — amendment 11 governs what the client ships, and a hex in prose ships nothing. `packages/ui/src/tokens.ts`, `packages/ui/test/helpers/colour-scan.ts`
- ⚙️ **Token-discipline scanner** — walks every client source tree (present and future — trees that do not exist yet are skipped, so a new client package is covered with no edit) for hex, `rgb()`/`rgba()`, `hsl()`/`hsla()`, `oklch()`/`oklab()` and `color-mix()` literals, exempting only `tokens.ts`. Proven against planted defects before the clean-tree assertion is trusted; it does NOT detect bare CSS colour keywords (`red`, `tomato`). The "future tree" clause stopped being a promise when `apps/web/app` and `apps/web/src` arrived: a `#b3261e` planted in `apps/web/app/globals.css` fails THIS package's suite, naming the other package's file — `apps/web/app/globals.css:13 #b3261e` — which is what the repo-relative hit paths were added for. `packages/ui/test/helpers/colour-scan.ts`
- ⚙️ **Base UI components** — Button, Chip, Card, Avatar, Dialog, Sheet, Table: BEM class names, colour only via `var(--gn-*)`, `type="button"` by default so forms are never submitted by accident, Escape-and-scrim close on the modal pair, and horizontal scroll contained inside the table wrapper rather than the page. `packages/ui/src/components/`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ⚙️ **Client `DataSource` contract & view types** — coordination amendment 10 pinned as code: `DataSource` (`ingest`, a typed `query` surface mirroring the server reads, `subscribe(workspaceId, afterSeq, cb)`), the `StreamItem` frames (amendments 13 + 19 — **five** members since Plan 6 Task 8: `event`, `purge`, `scope-revoked`, `caught-up`, `error`, the event member carrying a `LedgerEvent` so it holds the `workspaceSeq` the resume protocol is defined in terms of), and the view types every client renders from (`BoardView`, `ListRow`, `TicketRead`, `AttentionItem`, `UserPreferences`, …). Views consume only this interface and never import HTTP code. **THIRTEEN methods since Plan 6 Task 14's fix round**: `getTicketTypes(workspaceId)` was added because `GET /v1/workspaces/:workspaceId/ticket-types` had shipped in Plan 5 and no seam reached it, so the web shell hardcoded a four-entry ticket-type ladder and presented it as the workspace's own — see the New-ticket control below. It returns the domain's `TicketTypeDef` rather than a client view model, exactly as `getWorkflowDef` returns `WorkflowDef`, because the wire body already carries that type and there is nothing to rename. Scaffold only — the online HTTP + WebSocket source and the offline replica implement these same signatures later. `packages/app-core/src/`
- ⚙️ **Online stream subscriber** — `createStreamSubscriber({baseUrl, webSocketFactory?, random?})`, amendment 13's client half and the first implementation of `DataSource.subscribe`. It speaks the pinned frames verbatim and reconnects with exponential backoff from 500 ms capped at 30 s with ±20% jitter drawn from an injected `random`. The socket is an injected `WebSocketLike` seam — Plan 10's Tauri and React Native shells reuse it, and nothing in it assumes the DOM socket's deferred `close`. Two decisions carry their reasons at the site: the resume cursor advances from `caught-up`'s `seq` **and** from an event's `envelope.workspaceSeq` (never the per-entity `seq`), because `stream/hub.ts` sends `caught-up` exactly once per subscription and "only `caught-up` advances it" would guarantee unbounded replay on a long-lived connection — and it advances only after the consumer callback returns, since a returning synchronous callback is the only durability signal this seam has and over-advancing loses an event permanently while under-advancing costs a replay the consumer already dedupes. And `scope-revoked` is TERMINAL: delivered once, then the subscription shuts down, because reconnecting into a workspace you were just revoked from earns `not-a-member` forever. **It answers a TEXT `ping` with nothing, and that is a deletion rather than an omission (case 12.328):** the arms that declared `PingFrame`, parsed it and replied `{type:'pong'}` spoke a protocol no server implements — the heartbeat at `stream/routes.ts:319` is `socket.ping()`, a WebSocket PROTOCOL control frame the platform socket answers below JavaScript, and the server's message door answers `bad-message` to any text frame that is not a `subscribe`. Case 12.360 keeps them deleted, and 12.361 records the one residual a docblock carries and no test can: never inject a raw `ws` socket into this seam. `parseFrame` is hand-rolled rather than calling the domain's `serverStreamFrameSchema` — that schema is stricter than a client should be about id shape and a value import would resolve through `./dist` — and the schema is used as the test's ORACLE instead, pinning that everything it accepts this parser accepts. `packages/app-core/src/online/stream.ts`
- ⚙️ **Client submit factory** — `makeSubmittedFactory({actorId, origin, now?})`, the one door every claim the web client makes leaves through. It mints nothing itself: the ULID and the `clientTime` stamp come from the domain's `newSubmittedEvent`, so the web client, the desktop client and the offline replica cannot grow three ideas of what a claim is; the factory adds only the per-session half (who is signed in, which origin, which clock), and `serverTime`/`seq` stay absent by construction. Proven equal, field for field, to the domain builder's own output, and proven to mint claims the envelope door admits. `packages/app-core/src/submit.ts`
- ⚙️ **Provenance rendering** — `actorLabel`, `formatDate`, `formatDateTime`, `formatStateProvenance`: honesty contract 1 as code. A missing display name falls back to the raw actor id — never blank, never a fabricated "system". Every instant is rendered by the domain's one `formatIstInstant`, never by the device: the suite renders the same claim from `Pacific/Kiritimati` and `Pacific/Niue` and demands both the same words AND the IST words, so a formatter that is merely consistently wrong fails too. A day claim (`2026-08-30`) is shown as the day it already is rather than shifted through a zone it never had, an unparseable claim renders verbatim, and a calendar-impossible day claim renders verbatim rather than throwing at a rendering surface — the plan's unguarded `istDayBounds` call would have thrown on `2026-02-30`. The unfold text reads the SERVER clock, so no claimed clock can move a ticket onto a day it was never marked on. `packages/app-core/src/provenance.ts`
- ⚙️ **Pre-check state cache** — `ClientStateCache` / `createClientStateCache()`, a snapshot of what this client has SEEN, existing only to fuel the local rule-engine pre-check. It is never rendered as truth: `withCacheFeed(source, cache)` decorates a `DataSource` so ordinary `getTicket`/`getWorkflowDef` reads FEED it and stream frames invalidate it, and nothing is ever SERVED from it — a cache hit that short-circuited a read would put a snapshot on screen as a fact. Any stream event touching an entity DROPS the snapshot rather than patching it optimistically. Its one non-obvious member is `markChildrenComplete(parentId, children)` / `hasCompleteChildren`: `childrenOf` can only answer "the children I have seen", the parent-terminal invariant needs "all of them", and a partial child set reads to the rule engine as a parent with nothing open under it. Any ticket event voids EVERY vouch — targeted invalidation would leave a vouched-but-emptied child set behind, which is the pre-check's one route to a false pass. Nothing feeds a vouch yet; until a producer does (Plan 6 Task 7 or the board/list reads), moves into terminal states answer not-evaluated rather than passed. `packages/app-core/src/cache.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/cache-feed.ts`
- ⚙️ **Local pre-check** — `precheckEvent(submitted, cache, deps?)` runs the REAL `@gisik-nokma/rule-engine` in the browser build (spec honesty contract 2) for instant, named-reason refusal previews, plus `PARENT_TERMINAL_RULE` (`hierarchy.parent-terminal`) for the owner's reopen moment on `ticket.created`/`ticket.reparented`, read through the same registered payload schemas the server's door uses rather than a cast. It is advisory: the server stays the authority. The line it holds is that a `passed` is never manufactured out of ABSENCE — four gates downgrade to `not-evaluated` instead, each closing a way the cache's silence would otherwise read as a fact: an uncached ticket or definition; an unwalkable workflow-version chain at EITHER end (a missing intermediate both invents `stranded-state` refusals and silently disarms four-eyes, which skips entries it cannot resolve; a cached definition OLDER than the version the ticket's own snapshot names makes the forward fold a no-op, so the state resolves cleanly against the wrong vocabulary and the client evaluates the pre-publish rules the server has already replaced); an accept into a terminal state with no vouched-complete child set; and — since Plan 3 landed `ticket.created`/`ticket.reparented` as engine cases — a hierarchy claim of either type, entirely, because every engine accept for them is decided behind a ticket-type definition and this cache holds none. What survives for those two is the `hierarchy.parent-terminal` refusal, which reads only cached, server-served state. A roles-bearing `restrict-to` refusal downgrades too — memberships live server-side — while an assignee-only one stands, because the snapshot holds the assignee. `completeForPrecheck` stamps `seq: 0`, a position the ledger cannot hold, so the stand-in envelope is refused by the very door it would have to pass to become a fact. The standing canary that asserted the shared engine had no case for `ticket.created`/`ticket.reparented` FIRED on Plan 3 Chain A's landing (2026-08-31), which is what it was for; it is superseded in place by one aimed at the next drift — that the engine reaches a ticket-type door this cache cannot answer, pinned in both directions so it goes red whether the dispatcher cases are removed or the adapter starts answering `null`. `packages/app-core/src/precheck.ts`
- ⚙️ **Live query hook** — `useLiveQuery(source, workspaceId, queryKey, load, debounceMs = 200)`, the refetch loop every view hook in the web client rides on: load once, then re-run `load` (debounced) on every `event`/`purge` frame the workspace stream delivers. `queryKey` names WHAT is being asked for (the project id, for a board): a changed key clears the previous answer and re-asks, and an answer that arrives for a key already left is dropped rather than rendered. The clear is a passive effect, so the window in which the previous project's data sits under the new project's heading is bounded to the single render between commit and effect rather than lasting the whole load — shortened, not removed. The key drives the reload and the WORKSPACE drives the subscription — two effects, so a key change never re-opens the stream (`subscribe` resumes from cursor `0`, and each re-open would replay the workspace from the start). Deliberately refetch-based rather than patch-based — the stream is an invalidation signal and the server projections stay the one source of rendered truth; folding raw events into rendered state is Plan 10's replica, built on reducers. Speaks only to `DataSource`, never to a URL or a socket. `packages/app-core/src/views/use-live-query.ts`
- ⚙️ **Derived progress text** — `formatRollup(rollup)` renders `"5 of 8 done · 2 restricted"`. Counts are over ALL direct children with no visibility filtering: the denominator never shrinks to what the viewer can see, and children the viewer cannot see are NAMED in the restricted count rather than pretended absent (spec §6, invariant 5). `packages/app-core/src/views/rollup-text.ts`
- ⚙️ **Board view** — `Board` + `useBoard(source, workspaceId, projectId)`. Columns are exactly the attached workflow's states, empty ones included and blocked ones marked; a project whose ticket types sit on different workflows gets one group per workflow rather than a merged column set that would invent columns no definition declares. Grouping is a rearrangement and never a filter — the same tickets grouped two ways render the same tickets, and each column's count badge agrees with the cards under it. Cards fold their state behind "who · when" and unfold to `"marked Doing by Anselma Marak on 19 Aug 2026"`, falling back to the raw actor id when a claim carries no display name; a reopen renders its `cause` as "reopened — live work added" instead of an anonymous flip; a claim still in flight to the server carries a "claim in transit" chip, which is a CLIENT-SIDE `BoardCard` flag and not a fourth `IngestResult` status. Every instant goes through the domain's one `formatIstInstant`, guarded from two absurd zones by tests that hand the worker back exactly the `TZ` they borrowed. **These zone batteries are REGRESSION GUARDS, not live discriminations:** `formatIstInstant` and `formatDate` are constant +05:30 arithmetic over the UTC getters (`packages/domain/src/clock-skew.ts:51`, `packages/domain/src/ist-day.ts`) and read no local zone, so every zone prints one string against today's code and the zone loop cannot fail — measured 2026-09-02 against a `toLocale*` control in the same run that answered a different calendar day. The `TZ` probe establishes that the KNOB moves and nothing more. The teeth are the mutation: swapping in a device-reading formatter kills these cases, and each block now names its own kill count at the site. Pointing the hook at another project re-asks rather than re-rendering the last one. A test guard holds that no view module imports anything but react, the shared packages and its own relatives, and calls no network API — reading `from`, bare, dynamic and `require` specifiers, matching on whole package names rather than prefixes, and walking `src/views/` recursively. `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ⚙️ **List view** — `TicketList` + `useTicketList(source, workspaceId, projectId, currentUserId, filter?)`, with the pure model beside it (`countRows`, `groupRows`, `nestRows`, `visibleNodes`, `recentlyAssignedRows`, `columnLabel`). Per-user columns chosen and ordered, nested expand/collapse, *Recently assigned* pinned on top, and five groupings proven to rearrange the same rows with the same counts. **A default arrangement never wears the user's name, per HALF:** `prefs` carries `{columns, grouping}` as two `Chosen<T>` halves, there is deliberately no separate `prefsOrigin` prop that could be passed disagreeing with them, and the surface says *default arrangement — you have not arranged this project* when neither half was chosen and names the individual half (*default grouping — you have not chosen a grouping for this project*) when only one was. Touching a control transfers authority over THAT half only, so choosing a grouping on an unarranged project does not sign this client's stock columns with the user's name. **Grouping is a rearrangement, never a filter** — every row renders exactly once across the sections under all five groupings; **counts never shrink for visibility** — the summary reads the FLAT set, so neither a regrouping nor a collapsed subtree moves it, and a restricted child stays in the progress denominator with the restriction named; **nesting never hides a row** — a child whose parent is restricted renders at the root WITH the restriction named rather than dropped for want of somewhere to indent it. **The CLIENT owns grouping, and only because `TicketListView` is the complete row set**: the shipped `/v1/list` route groups per PAGE, so the online source asks for `grouping: 'flat'` and a TYPE tripwire on `keyof TicketListView` fails `typecheck` the day that view type grows a cursor. Every instant goes through `provenance.ts`'s one door onto the domain's `formatIstInstant`, guarded from two absurd zones with the probe that the ambient zone can move at all. **These zone batteries are REGRESSION GUARDS, not live discriminations:** `formatIstInstant` and `formatDate` are constant +05:30 arithmetic over the UTC getters (`packages/domain/src/clock-skew.ts:51`, `packages/domain/src/ist-day.ts`) and read no local zone, so every zone prints one string against today's code and the zone loop cannot fail — measured 2026-09-02 against a `toLocale*` control in the same run that answered a different calendar day. The `TZ` probe establishes that the KNOB moves and nothing more. The teeth are the mutation: swapping in a device-reading formatter kills these cases, and each block now names its own kill count at the site. **A NARROWED LIST NEVER WEARS THE PROJECT'S COUNT.** The hook takes an optional `FilterSpec` — hardcoded `{}` until the pagination cliff needed a way out — and the query key carries it, deeply order-stable, so a filter change re-asks rather than leaving old rows under a new control. When rows are narrowed the summary reads *N matching* and names the narrowing beside it, and the `· N done` half is DROPPED rather than recomputed: it is a fact about the project, and under the shipped narrowing it is structurally always zero. Renders through the shipped `Table` rather than a second copy of its markup. `packages/app-core/src/views/list-model.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-list.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ⚙️ **Preferences document hook** — `usePreferences(source, workspaceId)` → `{document, error, save}` (the exported `PreferencesResult`). **There is deliberately no `reload`:** an earlier shape returned one, nothing called it, and a reload bumps the generation counter without clearing `document` — so between the bump and the resolve `useTicketList` still reports `canSetPrefs: true` and a `setPrefs` landing there merges into a document the counter has abandoned. That the write wins anyway is an argument, not a measurement, and an export whose safety has not been measured is a liability. Deliberately NOT `useLiveQuery`: preferences are server-owned per-user rows (amendment 14) and no stream frame can carry one, so subscribing them to the ledger stream both wastes a refetch per ticket event and makes `use-live-query.ts`'s recorded F6 reachable — a debounced GET in flight when a `putPreferences` lands resolves afterwards under the same key with the PRE-WRITE document, and the arrangement the user just chose snaps back on screen while the server holds the new one. One generation counter orders loads against saves. `document` is `null` until it loads, never an empty document, because a whole-document PUT composed from a document this client has not read would erase every saved filter and every other project's arrangement — `useTicketList` refuses the write in that window and `TicketList` disables the controls so the refusal is visible. **Ruling P24 at the surface, BOTH of its asymmetries.** A project absent from the document reports `prefsOrigin: 'default'` and the view SAYS so, rather than presenting this client's stock arrangement as the user's own choice. The ruling's other asymmetry — a project present in the server's `listColumns` and absent from its `grouping`, or the reverse — used to be *erased* by a `DEFAULT_VIEW_PREFS` fill inside `mapPreferences` before any view was handed the document, which is what P24 forbids in as many words (*"reaching for `DEFAULT_VIEW_PREFS` there invents a preference the user never set … a project missing from `grouping` surfaces as **not set**, never as a default wearing a user's authority"*). **It is now represented rather than erased:** `ProjectViewPrefs`' two halves are each a `Chosen<T>` = `{origin: 'stored'; value: T} | {origin: 'default'}`, and the `default` arm carries NO value, so reading a value without first narrowing on `origin` does not compile — the ruling is enforced by the type checker rather than by convention. The stock values live in their own constants (`DEFAULT_LIST_COLUMNS`, `DEFAULT_GROUPING`); `DEFAULT_VIEW_PREFS` is now the statement that *neither half is set*, and fusing those two facts in one constant is what let a default be read as a choice. `mapPreferences` reports an absent half `{origin: 'default'}`; `toServerPreferences` contributes no key for such a half, so a grouping chosen alone is stored alone and a stock value is never PUT under the user's name; the pair round-trips a half-set project as half-set. One vocabulary for both scopes: `PrefsOrigin` is declared in `view-types.ts` and re-exported by `ticket-list.tsx`, and the project-level origin is derived from the halves by the single exported `prefsOriginOf` rather than stored beside them. `packages/app-core/src/views/use-preferences.ts`
- ⚙️ **My Work and the attention strip** — `MyWork` + `useMyWork(source, workspaceId)` and `AttentionStrip` + `useAttention(source, workspaceId)`. **The client's My Work and the server's `GET /v1/me/work` group on DIFFERENT AXES — a different model, not a rename:** the client is `{assigned, inTransit, contested}`, which is CLAIM STATUS, and the route answers `{open, active, terminal}`, which is STATE CATEGORY. All three server groups are this viewer's assigned work, so the whole route collapses into one of the three sections; `mapMyWork` records that the axis difference is adaptable and is *not* why `getMyWork` refuses. **`inTransit` is empty because there is no outbox, not because a read came back empty** — it IS the replica's outbox (Plan 10) and the web client is online-only (spec §5, whose same section places the outbox in the mobile and desktop replicas and nowhere else; Plan 6 carries it at line 23), so the emptiness is structural truth about this client's construction; the falsifiable half of that is asserted against the refusal register itself, which lists `ContestedClaim.title` and `ContestedClaim.claim.serverTime` and deliberately lists nothing about `inTransit`. A claim genuinely in flight is the `pending` view flag on one card, not a queue, and the in-transit card is badged distinctly so a claim the server has never seen cannot read as one it accepted. Contested cards name the RULE and repeat the engine's `reason` verbatim beside the claim's provenance. **The strip renders what exists and nothing else:** the union is discriminated over four kinds, the route emits three, and the flat list means an absent kind draws no heading, no count and no empty section — the word "Contested" appears nowhere when no contested item was given, and an empty strip renders no container at all rather than a reassuring all-clear on a surface that is structurally incapable of holding its fourth kind. Reasons are the SERVER'S, rendered verbatim (owner decision: the spec's "user's language" means named reasons, not localisation), so the strip shows the route's own `Needed by 2026-08-30 — due within 7 days.` rather than a browser re-derivation computed without the workspace's thresholds. `KIND_TONE`/`KIND_LABEL` are total `Record<AttentionKind, …>` maps, so Plan 10's fourth arm fails `typecheck` here rather than reaching a user as a blank chip. Both reads REFUSE today (`ServingGapError`) and both hooks let the refusal reach `LiveQueryResult.error` rather than substituting `[]` or three empty sections. Every date goes through the domain's one `formatIstInstant`, guarded from three zones — an instant in the strip, a DAY CLAIM in the due line — each zone checked against an expectation computed WITHOUT the code under test, behind a control that the `TZ` knob moves at all and a control that the fixture straddles the zones. **These zone batteries are REGRESSION GUARDS, not live discriminations:** `formatIstInstant` and `formatDate` are constant +05:30 arithmetic over the UTC getters (`packages/domain/src/clock-skew.ts:51`, `packages/domain/src/ist-day.ts`) and read no local zone, so every zone prints one string against today's code and the zone loop cannot fail — measured 2026-09-02 against a `toLocale*` control in the same run that answered a different calendar day. The `TZ` probe establishes that the KNOB moves and nothing more. The teeth are the mutation: swapping in a device-reading formatter kills these cases, and each block now names its own kill count at the site. **A FOURTH section, "Shared with me", now stands beside the three (case 13.176)** — the errands whose audience names this viewer, read off `MyWorkResponse.shared` and mapped through the SAME `mapWorkItem` as the groups, so a withheld project name degrades and a missing state name refuses by one rule rather than two. It renders whether or not it has rows, because a section that vanished when empty is indistinguishable from one that failed to load — and for the guest it exists for it is the only section that can ever be non-empty. **Every row is openable, and an earlier gate that withheld the button when the project NAME was withheld is GONE because its reasoning was false** — `routes/board.ts` gates on WORKSPACE membership and says so in writing ("a workspace member who is NOT on the project passes the gate and gets 200"), a guest IS a workspace member, and the guest arm of `visibleTicketsWhere` is the explicit clause, so their shared ticket is on that board; the gate withheld a working affordance from exactly the person the section is for. **The page's resolver now searches `shared`** — it turns a ticket id into `/w/:ws/p/:projectId?ticket=…` by looking the id up in the view, and searching only `assigned` and `inTransit` made every shared-but-unassigned row a silent dead click (no navigation, no error), which is the normal case for this section rather than an edge. **The project line also stopped being BLANK when the name is withheld** — `WorkItem.projectName`'s contract says the item "renders id-only rather than blank" and the renderer printed nothing at all, a PRE-EXISTING defect reachable before this change (a guest ASSIGNED an invisible-project ticket) and fixed here with one rule for every section, because this is where it becomes the normal case. `packages/app-core/src/views/attention-strip.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/my-work.tsx`
- ⚙️ **Ticket sheet — per-field attribution, both clocks, rule-driven transitions** — `TicketSheet`, with `HistoryList`, `TransitionDialog`/`transitionOptions` and the `EditorSlot` seam beside it. Every field row is a VALUE plus WHO claimed it and WHEN, because a value with no claim beside it reads as a property the ticket has — the laundering invariant 4 forbids — and a nameless actor is attributed by raw id through `actorLabel`, never blank and never "system". **The history tab shows BOTH CLOCKS on every line, labelled** (`claimed (device clock)` / `recorded (server)`): the claimed clock is the actor's claim about when and the recorded one is the system's record of when, both true in different senses, and a surface showing one under the word "when" asserts a moment the record never made. The fixture that proves it is skewed by THREE DAYS on purpose, so showing either clock alone prints a different string and either mutation dies; clocks seconds apart would make the whole battery vacuous. The zone is named once from the domain's `IST_CLOCK_NOTE`, and both clocks are checked unmoved from three zones against expectations computed without `formatIstInstant`. **These zone batteries are REGRESSION GUARDS, not live discriminations:** `formatIstInstant` and `formatDate` are constant +05:30 arithmetic over the UTC getters (`packages/domain/src/clock-skew.ts:51`, `packages/domain/src/ist-day.ts`) and read no local zone, so every zone prints one string against today's code and the zone loop cannot fail — measured 2026-09-02 against a `toLocale*` control in the same run that answered a different calendar day. The `TZ` probe establishes that the KNOB moves and nothing more. The teeth are the mutation: swapping in a device-reading formatter kills these cases, and each block now names its own kill count at the site. The three parent facts stay three — a RESTRICTED parent renders as existence-without-title, never as `At project root`. Transitions are prompted by the workflow's own declared rules (a comment box only where `require-comment` sits, a field only where it is not already claimed, an honest sentence where `require-evidence` meets a build that cannot upload), **where a wildcard edge and an exact edge both reach one target, the EXACT edge supplies the rules and declaration order does not matter — the engine's own resolution (`find(exact) ?? find('*')`), matched here under ruling P27 after `transitionOptions` was found resolving by declaration order while `precheck.ts` resolved correctly, one package holding two answers to one question**, the instant pre-check is advisory and prints nothing when it passes, and the SERVER's refusal renders verbatim in an `alert` beside — never instead of — a disagreeing preview. **The ledger payload is the SHIPPED spelling**, `{to, comment: {text}}` per `ticketTransitionedPayloadSchema`, not the plan's `{toStateId, comment: 'string'}` — which both the ingest door and the rule engine refuse as malformed, and which the local pre-check would put in front of the user as `malformed-payload: … at to: Required`. The workflow is read at the version the TICKET PINS, not at latest. `getTicket` REFUSES today (`ServingGapError`) and the refusal reaches the surface AS a refusal, under its own class, with the string `Loading…` nowhere — the plan's single `{read.error ?? 'Loading…'}` branch dressed a permanent serving gap as a wait that will end. **The History tab makes the same three-way distinction, and the ledger is where it matters most:** `useLiveQuery` leaves `data` null both while a read is in flight and after it rejects, so the tab's earlier single `history.data ?? []` drew an empty `<ol>` for pending, failed and empty alike — on the surface that IS the ledger, an empty list reads as *this ticket has no history*. Loading, failure and empty are now three renderings under three classes, with `error` tested BEFORE `data === null` because on a rejection both hold. The failure is worded and classed as an ERROR rather than a refusal: `getTicketHistory` is one of the six methods the online source ANSWERS, so it may succeed on a retry, and wearing `gn-ticket-sheet__refusal` would be a false claim in the CSS. The empty branch describes the ANSWER (*No entries came back for this ticket.*), never the ticket, because the online source returns `[]` on a 404 and a live ticket always carries at least its own `ticket.created`. **And the ledger is paged:** the shipped route defaults to 200 rows (`apps/server/src/routes/ticket-history.ts:78`), so the sheet asks with an explicit `HISTORY_PAGE_LIMIT` and says *Showing the first 200 entries — this ticket's history may go on past them* whenever the page comes back full. A full page is the only partiality signal on the wire (no cursor, no `hasMore`), so it over-reports on a ticket with exactly 200 entries — taken deliberately, because an occasional *there may be more* is a weaker claim than the truth where a silent truncation is a stronger one. It also UNDER-reports if the route's `MAX_LIMIT` is ever set below this number: the clamp returns a full-but-short page, the notice does not fire, and no client can tell — recorded at the site as the second reason the two numbers must be kept in step. No cursor paging is built. `PlainTextEditorSlot` is the honest read-only default over the domain's one `docToPlainText`: it renders and it composes nothing, because a build without Plan 7's editor cannot write a comment and says so by having no control rather than one that discards what is typed. `EditorSlotProps` is the seam Plan 7 fills — declared here with `doc: unknown` per amendment 12; **`packages/editor` declares no counterpart today**, and Plan 7's text declares `doc: DocJson | null`, which under this repo's `strictFunctionTypes` is not assignable to `EditorSlotComponent` (owner: step 12). `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-sheet.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/history-list.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/transition-dialog.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/editor-slot.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`
- ⚙️ **Attachment list — a held file and a Drive pointer are not the same thing** — `AttachmentList` / `AttachmentListEntry`, wired into the ticket sheet's details tab through an optional `attachments` prop. An UPLOAD links into this board (`/v1/attachments/:id`); a DRIVE REFERENCE links into Drive, wears its last-seen name and the day we looked, and says only that its contents are **unknown** — there is no "reachable" state on purpose, because a link that answered a moment ago may not answer now and one that failed may be fine, so `deleted`, `missing`, `no longer`, `available` and `verified` are all absent by test. The two fixtures share a filename deliberately: the name proves nothing, and a list keyed on it would show one row where there are two. The capture date goes through the one renderer rather than an ISO slice, which would report the UTC day and disagree with every other date on the sheet for five and a half hours every night. An absent prop renders NOTHING — not a heading with nothing under it, and no promise that there are no attachments. Plan 6 Open question 13 says `packages/editor` re-declares this union so the editor never depends on app-core; **correction (Plan 7 Task 16): it now does** — `AttachmentListEntry`, `packages/editor/src/components/AttachmentListItem.tsx:50`, landed by Task 12b — and Plan 7's `& DriveReference` spelling is the same six members this one inlines, both verified against the domain type in both directions (a dropped required member breaks the probe, dropping the optional `iconLink` does not), so the two texts describe one type rather than two. `packages/app-core/src/views/attachment-list.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/ui.css`; the counterpart is `packages/editor/src/components/AttachmentListItem.tsx` — see its own §9 bullet below.
- ⚙️ **The reopen-option flow — live children under terminal parents (owner decision)** — `submitChildEvent` / `reopenAndProceed` / `reopenStateOptions` (`src/flows/guarded-child-event.ts`) with `NewTicketDialog`, `ReopenDialog` and the wired `CreateTicketFlow`. A `hierarchy.parent-terminal` refusal is not an error: it is the moment the offer appears, at the moment of refusal, never silently applied. On acceptance the parent's `ticket.transitioned` — carrying `cause: {kind:'reopened-for-child', childTicketId}` and the SHIPPED destination spelling `to` — is ingested in its own awaited call BEFORE the child's, because the parent must be non-terminal by the time the child claim is evaluated; the child is the SAME envelope that was refused, since an interactive refusal records nothing. **The parent workflow's own transition rules still apply to the reopen:** its refusal surfaces verbatim and the child is never sent, which is what keeps the flow from being a permission escalation wearing a convenience's name. State options are STRUCTURAL only — non-terminal targets with a declared edge, rule-bearing ones included, so the server and not this client decides them. A refusal wears the name of whoever made it: `refusalOrigin` renders the instant pre-check as `instant check — ` (and submits nothing at all on that path) and only an answer that came back through `ingest` as `Refused by the server — `. A post-reopen child refusal reports both truths and compensates nothing away — the reopen is recorded history. **Known gap:** CLOSED by ruling P25 clause 1 — the offer needs the parent's state and workflow from `getTicket`, which REFUSED against the shipped server (`ServingGapError`) and now answers, so the flow builds the offer online. The refusal PATH is still pinned by 3.80 against a stubbed refusal, because what it guards is the flow's behaviour when a read it needs does not answer, whatever the cause, so online the flow renders the refusal and says the offer could not be built rather than guessing — case 3.80.
- ⚙️ **Online HTTP transport** — `createHttpClient(baseUrl, fetchImpl)` (`packages/app-core/src/online/http.ts`), the injected-`fetch` JSON transport behind the online `DataSource`. `credentials: 'include'` carries the better-auth session cookie; **404 answers `null` on a GET, and only on a GET** — every `/v1` READ route answers one frozen body for "no such thing" and "not yours" alike, so a client that distinguished them would be inventing a distinction the server refused to make, while on a POST or a PUT the same status is a refusal and answering `null` there let `putPreferences` report a save the server had declined (a member revoked mid-session); 204 answers `undefined` without parsing a body; every other non-2xx, and every non-2xx at all on a write, throws a status-carrying `HttpError`. `packages/app-core/src/online/http.ts`
- ⚙️ **Online `DataSource` — the HTTP + WebSocket implementation of amendment 10** — `createOnlineDataSource({baseUrl, streamBaseUrl?, fetchImpl?, webSocketFactory?, random?})`, exported from the `@gisik-nokma/app-core/online` subpath (the web shell consumes it. **The "nothing under `apps/web/app/`" clause this bullet carried after Task 14 is no longer true and is corrected rather than deleted**, because the distinction it was drawing still matters: the TRANSPORT is still constructed at one composition root — `grep -rn "app-core/online" apps/web/app apps/web/src apps/web/test apps/web/e2e --include="*.ts" --include="*.tsx"` returns 8 lines, of which `src/providers.tsx` is the only one that CALLS a `create*` function; the three page hits (`app/workspaces`, `app/invite/[token]`, `app/w/[workspaceId]/members`) import error classes and types only, so a page can name a refusal without knowing a URL. Nothing under `packages/app-core/src/views/` may import this subpath at all, enforced separately by a scanner.) It ingests through `POST /v1/events` with the contract's 1..100 batch cap enforced client-side, subscribes through Task 8's `createStreamSubscriber` at the STREAM origin, and answers **twelve of thirteen** (`getTickets` and `getTicket` joined the seven when P25 clause 1 closed their eight absences; `getMyWorkspaces` with P32/P38; `getMyWork` and `getAttention` with P39/P40 — `getMyWork` fetching the workspace roster beside its own body for `stateProvenance`, and `getAttention` joining `/v1/contests?open=true` for its fourth item kind) `DataSourceQuery` methods: `getMembers`, `getTicketHistory`, `getWorkflowDef`, `getPreferences`, `putPreferences`, `getBoard` and `getTicketTypes`. `getTicketTypes` re-raises its 404 as `HttpError` rather than answering `[]`: this route's 404 is `door(..., 'members-only')`, and `[]` would say *this workspace defines no ticket types* — a claim no real workspace can make, since Plan 4's bootstrap materialises the four built-ins as real `ticket-type.defined` events at creation. `getBoard` regroups the server's flat `columns[]` by `(workflowId, workflowVersion)` — the key `views/board.tsx` renders by, so a straggler column never joins a published definition's states — and joins the workspace roster off the board body's own `workspaceId` to name assignees, a client-side join over two shipped routes rather than an invented fact. Three signatures grew a `workspaceId` (ruling P25 clause 2): `getMyWork`, `getPreferences`, `putPreferences`, whose routes read it before the door. `getPreferences` re-raises a 404 as `HttpError` for the same reason `getBoard` does and one more: the server's answer for a person who has never saved anything is a 200 carrying the EMPTY document, so folding the 404 into defaults would show a revoked member stock columns and no saved filters and give them no way to tell that from their own settings. `packages/app-core/src/online/online-data-source.ts`
- ⚙️ **The one adaptation layer, and its ONE remaining REFUSAL (six until ruling P25 clause 1, four until P32/P38, one since P39 and P40)** — `packages/app-core/src/online/mappers.ts`, the only module that knows a server spelling. It carries three error classes with three owners: `MappingError` (the response is not the shape this client can read), `ServingGapError` (the response is right and the view type still cannot be filled honestly), and `UnexpressibleFilterError` (a REQUEST the shipped query language cannot say — a two-assignee filter against a singular `filterSpecSchema`). **ONE method refuses rather than returning a plausible view: `search`** — and it is the only DATA gap of the six, the one no join can close, because `routes/search.ts` destroys which column matched inside its own query. `getMyWorkspaces` ANSWERS since rulings P32/P38; **`getMyWork` and `getAttention` ANSWER since rulings P39 and P40**, and their absence lists were DELETED rather than emptied (an `Absence[]` with no entries is a refusal nobody can read). `getMyWork` refused over three facts and none was a missing capability: `WorkItem.stateName` was a read the route could always have made (`PROJ_TICKET_COLUMNS` has selected `t.workflow_id, t.workflow_version` since it was written — the claim in `read-models.ts` that a My Work card "names no workflow version" was true of the CARD and false of the ROUTE, and is corrected in place there); `ContestedClaim.title` was one join off a table that already stores `ticket_id`; and `ContestedClaim.claim.serverTime` was closed by DELETING the field, since a refused claim was never committed and never stamped. `getAttention` refused over one field, the contested kind's title, and closes on the same join. Its projects list is served by `read/visible-projects.ts`, which applies `resolveEffectiveRole`: owner sees every project (the shipped `canSeeResolved` switch exempts owner by name), every other role sees only `eff.projects`. **A partial workspace is the CORRECT answer, not a defect** — a project's NAME is itself a disclosure and is withheld for any project the viewer is not in, while its ID may already be known through the ticket surface. `WorkItem.projectName` follows the same rule one door over and is `string | null`: an item whose project is unnamed for this viewer renders id-only. **`getTickets` PAGES TO EXHAUSTION since ruling P41 (Door B)** — `mapListPage` maps ONE page and carries the server's `nextCursor` out, and `getTickets` follows it until the server says there is no more, assembling the complete `TicketListView`. It used to REFUSE any non-null cursor, which was honest and left the method unusable past 50 tickets; the refusal moved rather than vanished. The view type is UNCHANGED and that is the point: `TicketListView` means *the complete row set*, `views/list-model.ts` licenses client-side grouping on exactly that promise, and `test/views/list-model.test.ts` enforces it as a TYPE guard on `keyof TicketListView` — a cursor on the view would have deleted that guard and inverted the grouping decision in one commit, while leaving nothing able to act on the cursor, since `DataSourceQuery.getTickets` takes `(projectId, filter, grouping)` and has nowhere to put one (P30 holds; `data-source.ts` is not widened). The client SENDS `limit=200` — the route's validated `MAX_LIMIT` — so a large project costs a quarter of the round trips it would at the server's default, and the bound below is the client's OWN arithmetic rather than a coupling to a non-exported constant in another package. The loop is BOUNDED at `LIST_ROW_CAP` (2000 rows = `LIST_PAGE_CAP` 10 × `LIST_PAGE_SIZE` 200, the page cap DERIVED so the two cannot drift) and the bound REFUSES with `LIST_TOO_LARGE_ABSENCE` rather than returning what it has — a truncated set served as a project is the original defect one bound further out. **The cap is a disclosed assumption, not a measurement**, and both the constant's docblock and the absence's `owner` say so. The replica source is untouched: it reads the local confirmed set directly and has always answered the whole project. **The assembled view is NOT a snapshot and the loop says so** — ten requests are ten transactions, so a ticket made visible mid-walk below the passed cursor is absent until the next reload; disclosed at the loop rather than deduced, with an owner. A mid-loop 404 (access revoked between pages) is raised as `HttpError(404)` rather than reaching the mapper as "expected an object", the same conversion `getBoard` makes. **`getTickets` and `getTicket` refused until ruling P25 clause 1 and now answer** — their eight absences (`ListRow.typeRank`, `.epicId`, `.stateName`, `.assignedProvenance`, `.hasRestrictedParent`; `TicketRead.stateProvenance`, `.ticket`, `.title`) were served, not deleted, and the register's tests pin them as SERVED so a closed gap leaves a trace rather than a shorter array. Each refusal ENUMERATES the absent facts as data (`{field, wireKey, wireRoute, because, owner}`, exported as `SERVING_GAPS`), never as "not fully implemented", and every absence names who closes it and WHICH `/v1` body the server-side cross-check measures its key against — the contested absences named `GET /v1/contests` rather than the my-work and attention routes they used to blame, and that route grew the `title` and `projectId` they were waiting on. **The attention strip's fourth kind is now that client-side join**; My Work's contested claims are NOT, because "my claims" is viewer-scoped and only the server knows the viewer — ruling P40 put them on `/v1/me/work`'s own body as the additive key its docblock had reserved. `mapRollup` refuses a nonzero `uncategorizedChildren` (unreachable in production, and `RollupToViewer` has no room to admit an incomplete count) while an unnamed assignee degrades to `{id, name: id}` (reachable today — a guest is refused the roster route whole): refuse what cannot happen, degrade what can. Every OTHER substitution is gone — `blocked`, `cards`, a transition `cause` of an unknown kind and the three preferences containers are all REQUIRED on the wire, so each now throws `MappingError` naming its path instead of rendering "not blocked", "this state is empty", "no cause" or "you saved nothing". **The preferences pair is total in both directions (ruling P24):** `mapPreferences` reports a project half the server does not send as `{origin: 'default'}` rather than filling it with a stock value, and `toServerPreferences` contributes no key for such a half — so a grouping chosen alone is stored alone, a stock value is never PUT under the user's name, and a half-set project round-trips as half-set. `packages/app-core/src/online/mappers.ts`
- ⚙️ **Amendment 10 enforced, not asserted** — a scanner over the checked-in `packages/app-core/src` proving nothing outside `src/online/` imports the online module or touches `fetch`, `WebSocket`, `XMLHttpRequest` or `EventSource`. The import check reads all four specifier spellings — `from`, a bare `import`, `await import(...)` and `require(...)` — because anchored on `from` alone it answered "clean" for the two call forms, measurably, in the same run the `fetch` control reported a hit; the anchor stays (widened, not dropped) because this scan reads raw lines, comments included, and would otherwise report prose quoting the path as an import — measured as zero such lines outside `src/online/` today, so that half is a guard against the comment nobody has written yet rather than one that exists, and it is written down at that strength. It reports the FILES it walked beside the hits, so a clean tree can be told apart from a scan pointed at the wrong directory, and it is proven against planted defects before it is trusted. `packages/app-core/test/helpers/http-scan.ts`, `packages/app-core/test/no-http-in-views.test.ts`
- ✅ **`apps/web` — the Next.js 15 App Router shell** — the running web client, and the deliverable the rest of Phase 3 was blocked on. Routes: `/` (redirect to `/workspaces`), `/sign-in`, `/workspaces`, `/w/[workspaceId]/my-work`, `/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]` (Board | List tabs, attention strip above them, create flow beside them, ticket sheet via `?ticket=`). Both themes ship: `themeStylesheet()` is inlined in `<head>` and a pre-paint script re-applies a stored explicit choice, so an OS-preference default never flashes past a user's own decision. The root layout imports `@gisik-nokma/ui/tokens`, NOT the package root — the root is a barrel carrying `Dialog` and `Sheet`, both of which call `useEffect`, and reaching it from a server component fails the whole build; the subpath gives a server context the colours without the components. `apps/web/app/layout.tsx`, `apps/web/src/theme-toggle.tsx`, `apps/web/app/globals.css`
- ✅ **Passwordless sign-in, all four methods and nothing password-shaped** — phone OTP (two steps: request, then verify), magic link, Google, passkey; a `?next=` parameter carried into every callback. `src/auth-client.ts` is matched to the version the SERVER pins, not to the plan text: at `better-auth@1.7.2` the client plugin module exports `phoneNumberClient` and `magicLinkClient` but NOT `passkeyClient`, which lives in `@better-auth/passkey/client` — the client half of the same package `apps/server/src/auth/auth.ts` imports `passkey` from. The suite mocks the auth client wholesale, so it cannot fail on a member name; **`typecheck` is what checks those**, and it does — dropping `passkeyClient()` answers `Property 'passkey' does not exist` at the call site while the suite stays green. `apps/web/app/sign-in/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/auth-client.ts`
- ✅ **A refused read is rendered as the refusal** — every read in the shell checks `error` BEFORE `data`, and shows the `ServingGapError`'s own words — the field, why the shipped route does not serve it, and who closes the gap — carried without paraphrase. This is an adaptation, not the plan's text: the plan renders each read as `data === null ? <p>Loading…</p> : …` and the attention strip as `items={attention.data ?? []}`, and against the server that ships today both are false claims — four of the six refusing methods are called here, so the spinner never stops and `AttentionStrip` returns `null` for an empty list, which reads as "nothing needs your attention". A 401 is deliberately NOT a refusal: it means the viewer is unknown, and it redirects to `/sign-in`. **THAT SENTENCE WAS TRUE OF ONE PAGE AND WRITTEN ABOUT THREE, and is now true of all three** — the rule lived inline in `app/workspaces/page.tsx` as `message.includes('401')`, so an expired session on `/w/<id>/my-work` or `/w/<id>/p/<id>` rendered a refusal panel carrying `HTTP 401 for /v1/...`, telling a person their work *cannot be read* when the actionable truth was *sign in again*. It is now one exported predicate all three call (`src/expired-session.tsx`), ANCHORED on `HttpError`'s own `HTTP ${status} for ${path}` format rather than a substring, with a notice on screen for the frame before the router moves so the suppressed refusal does not become a spinner. It recovers the status from a MESSAGE because `LiveQueryResult.error` is a `string` and the transport's `HttpError.status` does not survive the hook; what that cannot see is named at the site. `apps/web/src/shell-refusal.tsx`, `apps/web/src/expired-session.tsx`, `apps/web/test/page-refusals.test.tsx`
- ✅ **The list can be asked a smaller question, and says so on every surface that answers it** — *Only tickets in progress* narrows the list, calendar and timeline (`stateCategories: ['active']`, applied server-side by `compileFilterSpec`, so it genuinely shortens the paginated walk rather than filtering rows already fetched). **It exists because `getTickets` REFUSES past a 2000-row cap and nothing could ask for less**, so a project past the bound refused for ever on every tab that reads the list. **The control sits ABOVE the tab arms and that placement is the property, not layout:** every renderer of the list lives inside `list.error === null`, so a control placed among them is reachable by everyone EXCEPT the person past the cap, for whom those arms render a refusal. **Every surface it touches discloses it, because a narrowed set presented as whole is the defect the cap's refusal exists to prevent:** the list summary reads *N matching* and names the narrowing (dropping the `· N done` half, which is a fact about the project and structurally always zero here); the page note names the surfaces AND exempts the board, which takes no filter and would otherwise be an unnarrowed set claiming to be narrowed on the default tab; the New-ticket parent picker names the narrowing beside itself, since a narrowed picker asserts *these are the tickets that could be a parent* inside a modal where the page's note cannot be read; and an epic filtered out from under its surviving children is named rather than rendered as its raw ULID. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-list.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/new-ticket-dialog.tsx`
- ✅ **The New-ticket control asks before it offers, and withholds itself rather than assert an absence** — the project page's create flow needs two facts it can fail to obtain and one it can fail to know, and each missing one used to become a silent claim inside a MODAL, where a sentence rendered on the page behind it cannot be read. (1) `parentCandidates` was `(list.rows ?? []).map(…)` and `getTickets` REFUSES against the shipped server, so the parent picker permanently offered *None (project root)* and nothing else — measured with the branch's own `stubServices({})` as `PARENT OPTIONS: ['None (project root)']` — while the Board tab on the same page rendered the very tickets it said did not exist, because `getBoard` IS served. (2) `typeOptions` hardcoded `epic`/`story`/`task`/`sub-task` as this workspace's types, so a workspace whose types differ (the server's own fixture answers `epic`, `chore`, `task`) got a picker offering four it does not have and `ticketTypeExists` refused the CREATE — a rejected write about the actor standing in for a read this build could not perform. The types now come through `getTicketTypes`, and the built-in ladder survives ONLY as the loading fallback, labelled on screen as what it is (Plan 6 Open question 7). (3) `currentUserId` was `user?.id ?? ''`, and `useCurrentUser()` answers null both while the session resolves and when signed out, so the button was offered to a signed-out viewer and a fast click in the load window posted an event with an empty actor — refused by `checkRolePermission`'s actor-identity check, which told the person about their PERMISSIONS when the truth was that this browser had not finished asking who they were. The flow is now mounted only on `useSessionStatus`'s `signed-in`, with the wait state and the signed-out state saying which they are. **That cost is PAID OFF, and the entry is corrected rather than left standing:** it used to read that `getTickets` refuses against the shipped server so the control is not offered at all and no ticket can be created from this page — *not even at project root* — with Plan 7 owning the gap. The premise was that the refusal is temporary and route-owned. It is not for the refusal a real customer meets: `LIST_TOO_LARGE_ABSENCE` is a bound this CLIENT chose and no route change clears it. Creation no longer gates on the list read (types still gate it, and that asymmetry is the point — a ticket needs a TYPE, not a PARENT); the dialog is TOLD the parent list is unavailable rather than handed an empty one, and **withholds the parent from the DRAFT and not merely from the screen**, because hiding a control does not clear the state behind it. **KNOWN AND NOT CLOSED HERE:** `TicketSheet` still builds submitted events from the same `currentUserId`, so the empty-actor window survives inside the sheet; closing it changes that component's contract. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/p/[projectId]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/test/project-create-gate.test.tsx`
- ⚙️ **`istToday()` — the one place this shell turns an instant into a day** — the device supplies the INSTANT, a fact it genuinely owns; `istDayOf` turns it into the IST day, and the device's own opinion about which day that is never enters. `now` is injected. Two wrong answers, two independent guards: `toISOString().slice(0, 10)` (yesterday's cell for five and a half hours every night) dies on the IST-midnight pair, and `toLocaleDateString()` (a different day per collaborator) dies on the three-zone loop. Stated plainly because the branch has corrected three overclaims of this shape already: the zone loop CANNOT discriminate against the current implementation — `istDayOf` reads only `Date.parse` of an offset-bearing string and the UTC getters — so it is a regression guard whose teeth are the mutation, and the `TZ` control below it proves the knob moves, nothing more. `apps/web/src/ist-today.ts`
- ⚙️ **Dev topology — two rewrites, and the third one's absence is tested** — Next proxies `/v1/*` and `/api/auth/*` to `SERVER_ORIGIN`, so cookies stay first-party and the online `DataSource` is mounted with `baseUrl: ''`. The WebSocket is dialled DIRECTLY at `NEXT_PUBLIC_SERVER_URL` because Next's dev server does not proxy protocol upgrades. A third rule for `/api/workspaces/*` was dropped when ticket reads moved to `/v1`; nothing serves that prefix, and a proxy rule for a path nothing serves fails differently through Next than direct, which is the confusing half of a 404. `test/next-config.test.ts` re-derives the served prefixes from `apps/server/src` on every run — comments stripped first, so a path merely DISCUSSED in prose cannot vouch for itself — and refuses a rule that matches none. `apps/web/next.config.ts`
- ✅ **Invite links — minting, holding, accepting, withdrawing** — spec §6's onboarding path, whole, over the four routes Plan 4 Task 14 shipped (Plan 6 Task 15 calls these an unowned surface gap; that sentence is stale and the paths were re-derived from `apps/server/src/routes/invites.ts` before a client line was written). An admin mints from `/w/[workspaceId]/members`; the link is `${WEB_ORIGIN}/invite/${token}` built by the SERVER, so this shell's route segment is one half of a contract already shipped. **The token is server-owned and hashed** — the raw copy leaves in the mint response and nowhere else, so the minted link renders exactly once under copy that says so, the pending list carries `expiresAt` and no token, and no "show it again" affordance exists because nothing could serve one. `GET /v1/invites/:token` needs no session, deliberately: the token IS the credential, so a stranger is told which workspace they are being asked to join before being made to authenticate — and the page distinguishes a session still LOADING from a signed-out one, which the plan's two-branch test does not. A dead link says used, withdrawn **or** lapsed, all three, because the door answers one frozen 404 for all of them and a holder must learn nothing about which. Withdrawing is an ordinary `invite.revoked` through `DataSource.ingest`, gated by `ADMIN_ONLY_TYPES` at the door rather than by a second check here. `packages/app-core/src/online/invites.ts`, `packages/app-core/src/online/invites-admin.ts`, `apps/web/app/invite/[token]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/members/page.tsx`
- ✅ **Founding a workspace** — a name field on `/workspaces` calling `POST /v1/workspaces`, routing the founder into their own workspace on the 201. It sits OUTSIDE the list's three branches on purpose: `getMyWorkspaces` refuses against the shipped server, so a form nested in the empty-state arm would be a form nobody could ever reach — and the person who cannot be told which workspaces they are in is exactly the one who most needs to found one. The empty state gained the second sentence it always needed ("— or create your own."). The name is submitted AS TYPED: trimming would repair a claim the person made and would make the door's own 400 unreachable for the one input it exists to refuse. `packages/app-core/src/online/founding.ts`, `apps/web/app/workspaces/page.tsx`
- ✅ **The people page, gated by the server's own 403** — the roster from `GET /v1/workspaces/:workspaceId/members`, and for owners and admins the mint form, the pending list and per-invite withdraw. **The role gate is the pending read's 403 (`INVITE_ADMIN_ONLY_BODY`), not a client-side role check** — an adaptation, because Task 15b gates on the caller's role from `/v1/me/workspaces` and `getMyWorkspaces` refuses whole, so no client on this branch can read it and a role-only mapper would be routing around a refusal. The door already answers 403 to a non-admin because an invitation's address is PII, so the read IS the question, asked of the thing that knows; the cost is one round trip a member makes and a 403 in the server's log. A member sees the roster, no invite machinery, and the door's own sentence rather than a silence they must interpret. The role picker offers no `owner` and the projects field is hidden on `guest` with the KEY omitted from the body, both matching refusals `inviteSentPayload` would otherwise make. `apps/web/app/w/[workspaceId]/members/page.tsx`
- ✅ **"Who can see this" — the visibility editor** — spec §6's audience control on the ticket sheet: a mode picker (workspace / project / explicit / inherit), a people picker over the roster on `explicit`, and `visibility.set` submitted through the ordinary ingest door. Two lines are OWNER DECISIONS, not wording: the workspace owner's read bypass is disclosed on **every** mode (a power the ledger cannot attribute is made honest only by saying it out loud, and showing it under `explicit` alone would imply the other modes exclude the owner), and an explicit list omitting its own author draws a lockout warning that **changes nothing** — the claim submitted is exactly the authored list (warning is not inventing). **The READ side is UNSERVED and the surface says so rather than guessing:** `TicketRead` carries no visibility field and the shipped ticket route's wrapper carries no provenance block, so the standing audience renders as an absence; drawing `inherit` would assert a mode nobody claimed. Its mount point is DARK against the shipped server — the sheet returns its refusal branch first and `getTicket` refuses — which is why the component's behaviour is proven directly and only the mount is asserted through the sheet. `packages/app-core/src/views/visibility-editor.tsx`
- ✅ **An empty board says words, not nothing** — `columns: []` from the server yields `groups: []` in the client, and that response is ordinary rather than exceptional (a workspace member not on a project-mode project passes the board's door and gets 200 with an empty board). Where the view used to render an empty `<div>` — loaded, refused nothing, said nothing, indistinguishable from still painting — it now renders `No tickets here yet — create one.` with an optional create affordance. The copy says "no tickets" and not "no columns": the server will not invent default columns for a project whose workflows the viewer may not read, so the client cannot tell "no work" from "none visible", and the sentence is true of both. `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`
- ⚙️ **`HttpError` carries the refusal BODY, and `refusalText` reads it** — every write door on this branch answers a refusal as a body and uses the status only to classify it (`INVALID_NAME_BODY` at 400, an `IngestResult` at 422, `INVITE_ADMIN_ONLY_BODY` at 403), so until this field existed the most a shell could show was `HTTP 422 for /v1/workspaces` — the shell paraphrasing a refusal it had never read, the same failure `shell-refusal.tsx` prevents for the six refusing READS. `undefined` is distinct from a parsed `null`: a proxy's HTML 502 must not surface as a `SyntaxError` naming neither the status nor the door. `refusalText` reads `message` and `reason` and answers `null` otherwise, never a manufactured sentence. `packages/app-core/src/online/http.ts`
- ⚙️ **Four entity-keyed `data-testid` hooks, for Plan 11 T13's E2E** — `workspace-card-${workspaceId}` and `project-card-${projectId}` on `/workspaces`, `board-column-${stateId}` and `ticket-card-${ticketId}` on the board and the list, disambiguating N structurally identical siblings by their own entity id. Genuinely new surface, not a house-style default: every other interactive element on this branch is still addressed by role/accessible-name (`apps/web/e2e/smoke.spec.ts`: `getByRole` ×19, `getByTestId` ×0), and these four are added only because role/name cannot tell two same-shaped cards apart. `packages/app-core/src/views/board.tsx`, `packages/app-core/src/views/ticket-list.tsx`, `apps/web/app/workspaces/page.tsx`
- ⚙️ **The Playwright smoke, off the gate twice over** — five cases over sign-in, the refusing workspace list beside its founding form, the board's card with its restricted-count line and attributed provenance, and both halves of the invite contract; `next dev` and a real Chromium, no API server and no database. It is kept off the gate by TWO independent facts — `test` is `vitest run` while the smoke is `test:e2e`, and `vitest.config.ts` includes `test/**` only — because one being edited away should not silently put a browser download on CI's critical path. **Every stub names the `apps/server/src/...:line` that registers the contract it stands in for**, which is the discipline this file exists under: the coordination doc records a missing route hiding behind this very smoke, so a stub with no named owner is that failure waiting to recur. The plan's navigation case is DROPPED rather than fixed — `mapWorkspaceRefs` is an unconditional throw, so `/workspaces` never draws a project link whatever a stub supplies — and replaced by one asserting that page refuses out loud and still offers the founding door. `tsconfig.e2e.json` puts the smoke on `typecheck` even though `e2e/` is excluded from the app's own tsconfig (which `next build` rewrites). `apps/web/e2e/smoke.spec.ts`, `apps/web/playwright.config.ts`, `apps/web/tsconfig.e2e.json`
- ⚙️ **`@gisik-nokma/ui/tokens` — a React-free subpath for server contexts** — the same `tokens.ts` the package root re-exports, reachable without the component barrel. Added because a Next server component needs `themeStylesheet()` and nothing else, and importing the root drags two `useEffect` components into a server module graph. `packages/ui/package.json`
- ⚙️ **The ONE local storage interface** — `SyncStorage` (`run`, `all`, `transaction`, `close`) over `SqlValue`/`SqlRow`, the single seam every offline replica writes through (coordination amendment 17): better-sqlite3 answers those four methods today, in tests and the dual-client harness; it is the seam op-sqlite (React Native, Plan 10 Task 16) and tauri-plugin-sql (desktop, Task 17) will answer, and neither exists yet, and `storageConformanceTests(name, make)` is the shared Vitest suite that keeps them honest — DDL/DML round-trip, commit-and-rollback, and no interleaving of concurrent transactions. JSON travels as TEXT: the replica owns (de)serialisation so adapters stay dumb. The native engine and the conformance suite ship behind the package's `./testing` subpath ONLY; the root barrel names its five exports one by one and a standing guard walks the module graph from it, so a Tauri or Expo bundle can never be handed a node-gyp addon or a test framework by the entry point. `packages/sync-client/src/storage/`, guard in `packages/sync-client/test/barrel.test.ts`
- ⚙️ **Local replica schema** — `migrateLocal(storage)` and `LOCAL_SCHEMA_VERSION`, idempotent DDL for the NINE replica tables — eight until ruling P36 added `snapshot_provenance`, a SIBLING table rather than a column on `snapshots` because `migrateLocal` runs `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` and nothing else, and `LOCAL_SCHEMA_VERSION` is written into `meta` and never read back by anything, so a new column would work on a fresh device and throw `no such column` on every existing one: `snapshot_provenance` (the state and assignment claims a snapshot-bounded bootstrap cannot otherwise carry), `events` (the visibility-scoped local ledger, keeping BOTH `seq` for entity replay and `workspace_seq` for the resume cursor, because dropping either forces the replica to invent an order it was never told), `snapshots`, the reducer-folded read models `tickets` / `workflows` / `ticket_types`, `contests`, the ordered `outbox`, and `meta`. The read models are folded locally by the domain reducer — they are NEVER a downloaded copy of the server's `proj_*` projections. `client_time` / `server_time` hold the server's Z-normalised ISO instants verbatim, so string order IS chronological order and a device-offset label must never be written there. No column carries a hand-written ULID CHECK: `ULID_REGEX` in `@gisik-nokma/domain` stays the one definition of that format, and a second one in SQL would drift. Table shapes only — snapshot policy and bounded replay belong to later Plan 10 tasks. `packages/sync-client/src/storage/schema.ts`
